```text
TASK-050 … TASK-055                       decomposition, architecture, toolchain, integration-authority,
                                          CI-evidence, and merge-executor findings
        |  the owner publishes its report inside its own write scope
        |  -> an authorized appender appends one entry keyed by its fact_id, before
        |     dispatch, outside tasks/**; no task record is written
        v
the ingress observer sees ingress_seq > cursor and dispatches TASK-013
        v
TASK-013 (orchestrator)
        |  creates one remediation task per responsible owner, or records the
        |  disposition when the responsible owner is the Orchestrator itself,
        |  performs the lifecycle transition, records the gate status,
        |  appends one ledger row per consumed entry, advances the cursor —
        |  all in one commit
        v
the named owner applies the fix on a new branch from the current integration branch
        |
        v
a new gate task records the next round of the same lineage and the gate closes
```

Validating roles report and revalidate; they never implement the fix. A validating role never revalidates in the execution context that produced the change. High and critical security findings block delivery until they are resolved or formally accepted by an authorized human. Remediation branches follow the same write-scope partition and the same contract change control as the original.

**A new return path opened at revision 29 and it has produced no finding yet, which is stated so the silence is not read as a result.** TASK-049's publication makes `LIN-RELEASE-EXECUTOR-REVIEW`, `LIN-RELEASE-EXECUTOR-SECURITY`, and `LIN-RELEASE-EXECUTOR-QA` live for the first time — **three lineages judging one artifact, in three roles and three LLM families**, which no earlier artifact in this graph has had. **No verdict exists in any of the three, so no finding exists, no disposition exists, and no remediation task was created or may be.** Three observations were recorded onto the four affected records for those rounds to **decide** rather than discover — the eleven declared-but-unexecuted live control-plane fixtures, the absence of any compiler so that typed invariants are checked only at run time, and the absence of a manifest because TASK-018 owns the root manifests and is not integrated. **They are observations routed for decision and are explicitly not findings**: authoring a finding is not the Orchestrator's authority, and `ACT-028` authored none, which is the discipline `ACT-024` used for the merge commit that carried no check runs and `ACT-025` for the degenerate control command.

**Both remediations have now completed the judgment half of their return path, and the architect half took three rounds and two remediations to do it.** At revision 27 **F-044-01, F-044-02, and F-044-03 are all `resolved`**, recorded by TASK-047 at `78359ae2e3dc6e97fb3d60f0b847b84abed08fa6`, closing `(TASK-046, r1)`, `(TASK-042, r2)`, and `(TASK-040, r3)` together; **F-041-01 remains `resolved`**; **F-041-02 and F-041-04 move from `partially resolved` to `resolved`**, by the F-044-02 and F-044-03 corrections that carried their residues; and **F-041-03 is explicitly not re-dispositioned**, because it belongs to the closed `LIN-CI-EVIDENCE-REVIEW` lineage. **Every one of those dispositions is a gate owner's**, transcribed by the Orchestrator, which formed no judgment on any of them. **No open finding remains anywhere in either integration-authority report, so no remediation task was created — and that absence is the correct outcome rather than an omission.** What the passing verdict routed instead is **implementation** work, which is a different kind of thing: TASK-048 and TASK-049, with six gate tasks. The architect half of round 1's report has now travelled report → remediation → report → remediation → report → **passing verdict** → **integration**, and at revision 28 it has **completed the loop**: the operator merged at `cf6333b10e628b3b61f3b7f8716b30923725067d` and all three cohort members are `done`. It is the second findings-return path in this graph to close completely, after the `devops` half at `f123c9a3`, and the first to do so across three rounds and two remediations. The superseded revision-27 clause read: it has **not** completed the loop, because the amendment is integrable and unintegrated and the merge belongs to the operator.

**One of the two remediations has now completed its whole return path, and that is new at revision 25.** F-041-03 was recorded by TASK-041, routed to TASK-043, remediated at `37a48249`, judged `resolved` by TASK-045 at `18cbdfad`, and **integrated at `f123c9a3`** — the first finding in this graph to travel the full loop from report to merged fix. **The integration re-disposed nothing**: F-041-03 was `resolved` before the merge and by a gate owner, and merging the artifact that remediated it neither strengthens nor re-confirms that disposition. The architect half has not completed the loop and is two rounds from doing so.

**Both remediations have now been judged, and the two rounds reached opposite outcomes on the same day.** At revision 24 **F-041-03 is `resolved`**, recorded by TASK-045 at `18cbdfad`, closing `(TASK-043, review, round 1)`. **F-041-01 is `resolved`**, and **F-041-02 and F-041-04 are `partially resolved`** with their residues carried by **F-044-02** and **F-044-03**, all recorded by TASK-044 at `6f7f0edb` inside a `changes-required` verdict. **Every one of those four dispositions is a gate owner's, not this role's**: the Orchestrator transcribed them, routed what they left open, and formed no judgment on any of the four. **A claim by an author is still not a disposition**, and the owners' claims stay transcribed on their records attributed to them.

**A partially resolved finding does not get a remediation task of its own when the round that partially resolved it has already assigned the residue.** F-041-02's residue is F-044-02 and F-041-04's is F-044-03, both stated by the reviewer, and both route to **TASK-046** with the finding that carries them. Creating a second remediation task for a residue already assigned to a named new finding is the scope error `ACT-008` and `ACT-010` each declined for A-004, A-101, A-102, and A-104.

**A returned dependency eventually has to be picked up by whoever owns it, and revision 27 is where one of the three was.** TASK-042 returned three things and TASK-046 carried all three forward. **The `tasks/**`-owned `gate_passed` narrowing is the Orchestrator's, and `ACT-026` applied it** — verbatim, pinned to `POST-GATE-MERGE-EXECUTORS.md` line 191 at `f148567d716c00d7a24783318c8d6d7031492e7b`, after four activations declined for four different reasons that are now all spent. **The `AGENTS.md` amendment is the user's and was not adopted**; no `agent/*` branch may author a governance or enforcement path, and no agent role's write scope contains it. **The `RepositoryPolicyAttestor` and the whole control plane are the user's and were not provisioned**; `ACT-026` read no policy surface and requested nothing. **A returned dependency that stays returned is not a defect in the return**, which is what rounds 2 and 3 each said; what changes is only who is now able to act, and for exactly one of the three that is this role.

**A remediation may return work rather than resolve it, and returning it correctly is a result rather than a failure — and round 2 has now said so.** TASK-042 returned three things: the exact `AGENTS.md` amendment text, which no agent role may author; the exact `tasks/**`-owned `gate_passed` narrowing, which only the Orchestrator may apply; and the `RepositoryPolicyAttestor` as an unresolved control-plane dependency that fails closed. **Round 2 judged the returns correct** — the attestor dependency "is the correct resolution shape for F-041-02 and does not falsely claim current constructibility", and activation "fails with `AuthorityNotActivated` until the returned tasks-owned correction is adopted and pinned". **F-041-02 is nevertheless only `partially resolved`**, on a defect in the component diagram rather than in the return. **`ACT-023` adopted none of the three**, and the reason for declining the one inside its own write scope is stronger than at revision 23: the amendment carrying it did not pass.

**One remediation task per responsible owner means exactly that, and revision 24 exercises the rule in the other direction.** TASK-041 recorded four findings naming **two** owner roles — three `architect` and one `devops` — and `ACT-021` created **two** remediation tasks with disjoint scopes, separate locks, separate lineages, and separate gate tasks. **TASK-044 recorded three findings naming exactly one owner role**, stating "All three findings are architect-owned. None is routed to the Orchestrator as its responsible owner", and `ACT-023` created **one**, TASK-046. **The rule is symmetric**: splitting one owner's three findings across two records would have produced two tasks with identical scopes contending on the same lock, which is as much a defect as folding two owners into one. **A finding's owner role is read from the report and is never reassigned, merged, or split for convenience**, and each of the three owner lines was checked individually rather than accepted from the report's summary sentence.

**A `review` finding is not a `security` finding, and the two have different closure rules.** F-041-01, F-041-02, and F-041-03 were High and F-041-04 Medium, and F-044-01, F-044-02, and F-044-03 are Medium and recorded blocking; every one is recorded by a `review` gate. They block integration until a later round of their lineage records a passing verdict, and they need no human acceptance. The clause about formal human acceptance applies to findings recorded by the `security` gate, which is owned by TASK-010 and has recorded none. The distinction matters here because **F-041-01 is a finding *about* formal acceptance** — that the executor's admission predicate would honour a formal acceptance of the wrong kind — and a reader could otherwise conflate the mechanism the finding describes with the mechanism that would close it.

## Operational notes for each next owner

1. From the primary checkout, run `scripts/orchestration/create-worktree.ps1 -TaskId <id> -Role <role> -Llm <llm>`. Reuse an existing worktree when the task already has one.
2. Start the assigned CLI inside the returned worktree path and run `scripts/orchestration/claim-task.ps1` before editing.
3. Before handoff, run `scripts/orchestration/validate-write-scope.ps1 -IncludeWorkingTree -BaseRef <scope_validation_base>`, using the value your record declares — the **immutable branch point of your own branch**, never a review-diff base and never `origin/main` by default. If your record declares the reproducible expression rather than a hash, resolve it first with `git merge-base HEAD <branch_point_of>` and record the resolved value in your handoff. See "Task baselines" above.
4. Commit on the task branch. Push the task branch and open or update a pull request; never push `main`. If publication is unavailable and your `publication_class` is `bootstrap`, record `publication: local-only` with the reason so the Orchestrator can transcribe it accurately. If your `publication_class` is `runtime`, an unavailable remote is a `blocked` outcome, not a `local-only` success.
5. Run `scripts/orchestration/release-task.ps1`.
6. Record the handoff where your own write scope allows — the commit message, the pull request description, and your role's report artifact. **Do not move your task record and do not edit its `status` field.** The Orchestrator performs every task-record transition under TASK-013. Publishing your own artifact is how you wake it; you never write to `tasks/`.

**Revision 29 adds one thing to step 7 and changes nothing else about it, and the thing it adds is a refusal.** The module that would eventually perform the integration-to-`main` half of this step now **exists**, at `9fb2eb0ca7c02101fd067452824e2612fda5cc0c`. **Step 7 is still automated by nothing.** TASK-049's own source is what enforces that: `admit` returns `AuthorityNotActivated` while any `MergeExecutorActivationRecord` member is absent, and five of the seven are, so the code that could merge refuses to. **Its own pull request 30 is subject to this step unchanged** — it may not be merged until TASK-053 and TASK-054 both close at a passing verdict, which is the plainest available demonstration that building a merge executor grants its author nothing. **No Orchestrator activation may perform any merge and `ACT-028` performed and simulated none.** **The asymmetry named at revision 28 is unchanged in substance and sharper in form**: TASK-049, which would automate the integration-to-`main` merge, has published a dormant module; TASK-048, which would automate the task-to-integration merge that has now been performed manually three times, is still `blocked`. The superseded revision-28 statement read: **Revision 28 records step 7 executed for the second time in this graph, and changes nothing else about it.** The operator merged pull request 28 at `cf6333b10e628b3b61f3b7f8716b30923725067d`, and the ADR-0041 cumulative unit behaved exactly as `INTEGRATION-STRATEGY.md` prescribes: **one squash-shaped content merge for TASK-046, zero commits for TASK-042 and TASK-040, and `lineage-subsumed` lifecycle evidence for both**, verified by ancestry and by tree-object equality rather than asserted. **Pull requests 22 and 25 were not merged separately and did not need to be** — GitHub closed them as `MERGED` against their own heads because ancestry made them so. **No Orchestrator activation may perform that merge and `ACT-027` did not; it consumed the resulting fact.** **The asymmetry the revision-27 statement named has half-resolved and is worth restating in its current form**: TASK-049, the executor that would eventually automate the integration-to-`main` step, is now `ready` **because a manual instance of the task-integration step was performed for it** — and it is `ready` only to write a module that cannot run. TASK-048, which would automate the very step just performed, is still `blocked`. **The step that would automate the merge is still not automatable, and integrating its specification did not change that.** The superseded revision-27 statement read: **Revision 27 adds one thing to step 7 and changes nothing else about it.** Pull request 28 may now be merged by the operator, because TASK-046's only pre-merge gate closed at `approved`. Under the approved `INTEGRATION-STRATEGY.md` that single merge is the whole cumulative content unit — `5e5fc8f` and `e33a62be` are ancestors of `f148567d` — so **TASK-042 and TASK-040 reach `integrated` through the same merge by lineage subsumption and receive no commit of their own**, and merging pull requests 22 and 25 separately is neither required nor prescribed here. **No Orchestrator activation may perform that merge and `ACT-026` did not.** **The two executors that would eventually automate this step now have task records, and neither may be started**: TASK-048 and TASK-049 are `blocked` on the very merge described here, so the step that would automate the merge is itself blocked behind a manual instance of it. That is stated plainly rather than presented as a contradiction — it is what "conditionally authorized and not yet built" actually looks like.

7. **No owner merges anything.** Opening or updating a pull request is where an owner's authority ends. The merge into `integration/autonomous-runtime`, and the merge of that branch into `main`, are separate externally visible operations performed by the operator, only after every gate in the target's `pre_merge_gates` is closed, and each produces its own `branch_integrated` ingress fact. **Revision 25 is the first time the step has actually been performed for a task branch**: TASK-043's only pre-merge gate closed at `approved`, and **the operator merged pull request 24 at `f123c9a3` on 2026-08-07T11:43:28Z**, producing the `branch_integrated` fact `ACT-024` consumed. **`ACT-024` consumed that fact and did not perform, request, or simulate the merge**, and no Orchestrator activation may perform one. The superseded revision-24 statement read: **Revision 24 is the first time that condition has been met for a task branch**: TASK-043's only pre-merge gate closed at `approved`, so **pull request 24 may be merged by the operator** — and **no Orchestrator activation may perform it, and `ACT-023` did not.** **An operator performing this step by hand is the opposite of evidence that an executor exists.** **`HUMAN-004` is approved and it does not change this step.** It authorizes two executors that do not exist, conditionally, after an amendment is approved and an implementation is independently reviewed and security-validated; **rounds 1 and 2 of `LIN-INTEGRATION-AUTHORITY-REVIEW` each recorded `changes-required` and each denied implementation authorization explicitly**, so until a round passes and those executors are built and validated, this step is unchanged, and no task may act as though the decision had already been implemented. The decision says so itself: existing pull requests continue to obey their current gates, and it "does not retroactively authorize a gate bypass".

Steps 1 through 5 are what TASK-017 automates for runtime-dispatched work, including step 4's publication and pull-request outcome. They remain the manual procedure for the human-launched CLI sessions that build the runtime itself. **Step 7 is automated by nothing today, and `HUMAN-004`'s approval does not change that sentence** — it names the two components that would eventually automate it and the four conditions each must clear first.
